That is a brilliant catch, and it's actually an amazing demonstration of how Genetic Algorithms work (and how they find loopholes)!\n\nHere is exactly why it returned `0 light, 0 heavy`:\nWhen the app first boots up, there is no delivery demand generated yet (all cells have 0 demand). The old Genetic Algorithm's fitness function rewarded the drones for covering demand, but mathematically **penalized** them for spending the budget. \n\nBecause the demand coverage was exactly `0.0` across the entire board, the GA realized that no matter what drones it bought, its score wouldn't go up. It figured out that the mathematically optimal move was to buy **absolutely nothing** to avoid the cost penalty! The AI basically chose to go on strike.\n\n<truncated 709 bytes>"}

{"step_index":1365,"source":"MODEL","type":"PLANNER_RESPONSE","status":"DONE","created_at":"2026-05-11T19:22:03Z","content":"Good catch! This was an extremely confusing visual artifact caused by how `Plotly` draws graphs.\n\nIn standard mathematics (and Plotly), `(0,0)` is at the **bottom-left** corner. However, in Python lists, matrices, and image data, row `0` is always at the **top**. To compensate for this, the old map rendering code was manually plotting elements upside-down so the grid would physically look correct, but it left the Plotly Y-axis labels running from `0` (bottom) to `10` (top)! This meant when the log said `Row 3`, you visually traced to the label `3` near the bottom, even though the drone was actually at the top of the map.\n\n**How I fixed it:**\nI completely ripped out the manual upside-down plotting math and instead commanded Plotly's rendering engine to explicitly invert the Y-axis (`range=[10, 0]`). \n\nNow, `Row 0` is visually at the top-left (just like the Demand Heatmap matrix), and `Row 9` is at the very bottom. The grid visually matches the coordinates exactly 1:1!\n\n<truncated 251 bytes>"}

{"step_index":1368,"source":"MODEL","type":"PLANNER_RESPONSE","status":"DONE","created_at":"2026-05-11T19:29:55Z","content":"The delivery assignment logic operates using a **Nearest-Available Greedy Heuristic** combined with a **Payload Capacity Check**. \n\nHere is the exact step-by-step logic that happens under the hood when a new delivery is generated:\n\n1. **Find Idle Drones:** The system gathers a pool of all drones that are currently `idle` (meaning they are sitting at a Hub and not flying). If all drones are busy, the delivery stays in the `pending` queue.\n2. **Nearest-Drone Search:** For the current delivery, the simulator calculates the **Manhattan distance** between the delivery's `pickup` coordinate and the current location of every idle drone. It temporarily selects the closest idle drone to minimize flight time.\n3. **Payload Capacity Check:** \n   - It checks the weight of the package against the maximum payload of the selected drone (Light Drones can carry up to **2.5 kg**, Heavy Drones up to **8.0 kg**).\n<truncated 810 bytes>"}

{"step_index":1459,"source":"MODEL","type":"PLANNER_RESPONSE","status":"DONE","created_at":"2026-05-11T19:47:06Z","content":"Here's exactly what the old bug was and how it's fixed:\n\n### The Bug\nThe old `_reroute_affected()` method used `goal = drone.route[-1]` — the last cell in the full round-trip route. Since the route is always `Hub → Pickup → Dropoff → Hub`, `route[-1]` was **always the hub**. So when a no-fly zone blocked D2's path, it just flew straight home, abandoning Delivery 1 entirely. Then it became idle and got assigned a completely new delivery.\n\n### The Fix\nThe reroute logic is now **lifecycle-aware**. It checks the delivery status to decide what to do:\n\n| Drone Phase | Delivery Status | Reroute Behavior |\n|---|---|---|\n| Flying to pickup | `assigned` | Rebuild: `current_pos → pickup → dropoff → hub` |\n| Carrying package | `in_transit` | Rebuild: `current_pos → dropoff → hub` |\n| Returning home | `returning` | Rebuild: `current_pos → hub` |\n\nHit **\"🔄 Reset\"** in the browser and you'll see D2 now properly reroutes around the no-fly zone while keeping its original delivery!"}
